The search for "LogicForge game OR LogicForge learn programming" returned the following summary:
It appears there is some ambiguity regarding the term **"LogicForge,"** as it refers to different things depending on the context:

*   **Logic Puzzle Game:** There is a mobile game titled **"LogicForge"** (available on the Google Play Store) that is a premium, neon-styled logic puzzle platform. It focuses on solving deduction riddles, cracking logic matrices, and working with boolean circuits rather than teaching actual programming languages[1].
*   **Education & Coding:** There is a separate entity/platform called **LogicForge** that provides interactive coding labs, proctored assessments, and AI analytics for those learning to program[2].
*   **IT & Hosting Services:** "LogicForge" is also the name of a UK-based company that operates **LogicServers.com**, which provides game server hosting for various titles (such as *Minecraft* and *ARK: Survival Evolved*) and offers custom software development services[3][4][5].
*   **Unity Tool:** There was also a visual scripting tool for the Unity game engine formerly known as "Logic Forge"[6][7].

### If you are looking for games to learn programming:
If your goal is to learn how to code through gaming, there are many established platforms and games designed specifically for that purpose:

*   **CodinGame:** A popular platform where you solve programming challenges and puzzles to level up your skills in various languages[8][9][10].
*   **CodeCombat:** An adventure-based game that teaches Python and JavaScript by having players write real code to control characters[9][11].
*   **Tynker:** Offers a wide range of interactive coding games, moving from block-based coding to real-world languages like Python and JavaScript[11].
*   **Human Resource Machine / Shenzhen I/O:** These are "programming-adjacent" puzzle games that teach logic, assembly, and flow control in a game environment[12][13][14].
*   **Codédex:** An interactive platform that turns the learning process into a quest-based adventure with XP and badges[15].

2: 
3: > **Modern Three.js & Digital Logic Reimagining of the 1984 Classic**  
4: > *Created with pair programmer Jack & assistant Esperanza.*
5: 
6: ---
7: 
8: ## ⚡ How to Resume After Restart
9: 
10: When you boot back up after the storm, simply open a terminal in this directory and run:
11: 
12: ```bash
13: cd /home/jack/Desktop/ROBOT_ODYSSEY
14: npm run dev
15: ```
16: 
17: Then open your browser to: **[http://localhost:5173/](http://localhost:5173/)**
18:

20: 
21: ## 🕹️ Quick Controls & Shortcuts
22: 
23: | Action | Control |
24: |---|---|
25: | **Start / Stop Simulation** | `Spacebar` or click **`RUNNING / PAUSED`** in top nav |
26: | **Step 1 Tick Forward** | `.` (Period) or click **`⏭ STEP`** (when paused) |
27: | **Manual Remote Drive** | **`W`**, **`A`**, **`S`**, **`D`** or **Arrow Keys** (or on-screen D-Pad) |
28: | **Resize Split Screen** | Click & drag the central divider handle |
29: | **Connect Wires** | Drag from any **OUT** pin (right) to an **IN** pin (left) |
30: | **Cut / Remove Wire** | **Left-Click** or **Right-Click** any wire (turns red on hover) |
31: | **Unplug Pin** | Click an **IN** terminal to pop its incoming wire off |
32: | **Burn to Microchip** | Click **`🔥 BURN TO CHIP`** in the motherboard toolbar |
33: | **Reset Flux to Spawn** | Click **`RESET BOT`** in top nav |
34:

36: 
37: ## 🛠️ Features Implemented
38: 
39: ### 1. 3D World (Three.js)
40: * **Robot Flux:** Chamfered cybernetic chassis, 4 contact bumpers with cyan LED collision detection, rotating radar dish, articulated front grabber claws, and 4 directional thrusters with dynamic plasma flame plumes and floor illumination.
41: * **Chamber Environment:** Dark metallic grid arena with volumetric lighting, industrial hazard obstacles, and an energy crystal core on a pedestal with beacon detection and victory chimes.
42: * **Manual Override D-Pad:** On-screen translucent remote control + keyboard WASD support that blends seamlessly with circuit logic.
43: 
44: ### 2. The Interactive Motherboard & Wiring System
45: * **Full-Height Workspace:** Fixed container layout giving the breadboard full vertical and horizontal screen dimensions.
46: * **Dynamic Bezier Wires:** Fluid springy curves with magnetic snapping.
47: * **Live Current Animation:** When a wire is carrying a `1` (HIGH), animated neon cyan particle dashes race along the line at 60 FPS.
48: * **Effortless Wire Removal:** 
49:   * Hover over any wire to turn it glowing red; click anywhere along its 24px wide hit area to snip it.
50:   * Click any connected `IN` terminal pin to immediately pop its wire off.
51:   * Right-click any pin to disconnect all attached lines.
52: 
53: ### 3. IC Chip Component Library
54: * **Logic Gates:**
55:   * `7408 AND Gate` (2-Input, 1-Output)
56:   * `7432 OR Gate` (2-Input, 1-Output)
57:   * `7404 NOT Inverter` (Inverts 0 to 1; constant 1 output when unconnected)
58:   * `7486 XOR Gate` (Exclusive OR)
59: * **Memory & Timing:**
60:   * `74279 RS Flip-Flop` (Sequential memory latch for patrolling)
61:   * `555-D Delay Buffer` (Signal delay node)
62: * **AI Autopilot Co-Processor:**
63:   * `CORTEX-1 AI` (Heuristic vector engine that automatically navigates Flux toward the crystal, with hard-wired `OVERRIDE` input for discrete safety interlocks).
64: 
65: ### 4. Innovation Lab ("Burn to Microchip" Compiler)
66: * No page reloads or separate tabs—integrated right into the workbench.
67: * Click **`🔥 BURN TO CHIP`** to inspect all placed chips and wires.
68: * Automatically detects external inputs (from sensor wires) and outputs (to actuator wires).
69: * Custom naming, silkscreen code generation, and live 3D DIP IC package preview.
70: * Clicking **`⚡ FABRICATE`** encapsulates the sub-circuit and creates a reusable, socketable chip in your **IC TOOLBOX** marked with `★ YOUR_CODE`.
71: 
72: ### 5. Simulation Control Deck
73: * **Play / Pause:** Instant time-freeze (`Spacebar`) holding voltages and physics in place for visual inspection.
74: * **Frame Stepper:** Advance simulation by 1 single clock cycle (`.`) for debugging.
75: * **Speed Select:** `0.25x`, `0.5x`, `1.0x`, `2.0x`.
76: 
77: ### 6. Procedural Synthesizer (Web Audio API)
78: * Zero external audio files or downloads.
79: * Synthetic procedural relay clicks, wire plug snaps, wire cut zaps, pink-noise thruster rumbles, metal bumper pings, and harmonic victory chimes.
80:

1: # 🛰️ LOGICFORGE ARCHITECTURE & REUSABLE TEMPLATE
2: ## *The Foundational Blueprint for Logic-Driven Autonomous Games (LOGICFORGE-V2 ➔ SPACE_MINER_BOTS)*
3: 
4: ---
5: 
6: ## 🌟 1. Core Architectural Philosophy
7: 
8: LogicForge is built on a clean, zero-bloat, client-side architectural model. It requires **no server-side game loops, no heavy databases, and no massive UI frameworks**. 
9: 
10: ```
11:                                ┌────────────────────────────────┐
12:                                │       VITE BUILD FACTORY       │
13:                                │ (Human-readable src/ blueprints│
14:                                │        + Three.js engine)      │
15:                                └───────────────┬────────────────┘
16:                                                │ (npm run build)
17:                                                ▼
18:                                ┌────────────────────────────────┐
19:                                │      STATIC PRODUCTION DIST    │
20:                                │   - index.html   - robots.txt  │
21:                                │   - assets.css   - assets.js   │
22:                                └───────────────┬────────────────┘
23:                                                │ (Nginx / Cloud)
24:                                                ▼
25: ┌────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
26: │                                CLIENT BROWSER SANDBOX                                  │
27: │                                                                                        │
28: │   ┌───────────────────────┐   ┌────────────────────────┐   ┌───────────────────────┐   │
29: │   │     3D WORLD SIM      │   │     CIRCUIT ENGINE     │   │     MOTHERBOARD UI    │   │
30: │   │   Three.js + Physics  │◀──│   Logic Ticks (60Hz)   │──▶│    Dynamic SVG Wires  │   │
31: │   │   Procedural Entities │   │  4-Pass Signal Relax   │   │  Zoom/Pan Canvas + ICs│   │
32: │   └───────────────────────┘   └───────────┬────────────┘   └───────────────────────┘   │
33: │                                           │                                            │
34: │                      ┌────────────────────┴───────────────────┐                        │
35: │                      ▼                                        ▼                        │
36: │          ┌───────────────────────┐                ┌───────────────────────┐            │
37: │          │  SILICON CHIP BURNER  │                │    STORAGE MANAGER    │            │
38: │          │   Circuit Compiler    │                │  localStorage Battery │            │
39: │          │  Encapsulate to 1 IC  │                │  Campaign, CR, Wiring │            │
40: │          └───────────────────────┘                └───────────────────────┘            │
41: └────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
42: ```
43: 
44: ---
45: 
46: ## ⚙️ 2. The Five Engine Pillars
47: 
48: ### Pillar I: The Circuit Simulation Core (`engine.js`)
49: * **Pure Discrete State**: No analog differential solver overhead needed. Inputs and outputs evaluate boolean states (`HIGH` / `LOW`) or discrete stepped values.
50: * **Multi-Pass Relaxation**: Signal propagation uses 4 passes per tick to ensure sequential logic (latches, flip-flops, ripple counters) resolves loops without locking the main thread.
51: * **Sensor / Actuator Bus**: The circuit engine is decoupled from the 3D physics. It only talks to:
52:   * `sensors` (`bumper_n/s/e/w`, `radar_ping`, `item_detect`, `clock_tick`)
53:   * `actuators` (`thrust_n/s/e/w`, `grabber`, `aux_light`, `beacon_ping`)
54: 
55: ### Pillar II: Procedural 3D World Engine (`scene.js`, `robot.js`)
56: * **Zero External 3D Models**: All entities (bots, refinery, crystal beacons, elevator) are constructed mathematically from Three.js primitives (`CylinderGeometry`, `BoxGeometry`, `TorusGeometry`, canvas textures).
57: * **Super Lightweight Memory Footprint**: Uses only **~56 MB** of RAM and loads in **under 200 milliseconds**.
58: * **Layered Coordinate Spaces**: Multi-floor / multi-level spatial partitioning (Level 1, Sub-Level 2, Sub-Level 3).
59: 
60: ### Pillar III: Motherboard Breadboard & Wire Overlay (`board.js`)
61: * **Unscaled SVG Overlay**: Breadboard wires are drawn as vector SVG `<path>` bezier curves across a single unscaled overlay layer.
62: * **Zoom-to-Cursor & Pan System**: Only the internal chip canvas transforms (`scale` + `translate`), while input/output terminal banks remain anchored.
63: * **Tactile Interactions**: Drag-to-wire, pin unplugging, right-click cutting, double-click silicon inspector.
64: 
65: ### Pillar IV: Silicon Fabrication Compiler (`burner.js`)
66: * **"Burn to Microchip"**: Takes whatever sprawling circuit is currently breadboarded, maps sensor wires to input pins and actuator wires to output pins, encapsulates internal traces into silicon, and fabricates a single custom IC added to the toolbox dock.
67: * **Hierarchical Nesting**: Custom chips can be placed inside *other* circuits and re-burned into even higher-level chips!
68: 
69: ### Pillar V: Client-Side Battery Save (`storage.js`)
70: * **Zero-Database Persistence**: Uses `localStorage` as a virtual ROM cartridge.
71: * **State Encapsulation**:
72:   * Campaign mission progression & completion flags.
73:   * Economy balance (Credits) & unlocked robot fleet.
74:   * Custom burned microchip blueprints.
75:   * Exact chip positions and wire routings for every robot.
76: 
77: ---
78: 
79: ## 🚀 3. Adapting to *SPACE_MINER_BOTS*
80: 
81: Here is how the LogicForge architecture translates directly into the next project:
82: 
83: | LogicForge Concept | *Space Miner Bots* Evolution |
84: | :--- | :--- |
85: | **Arena Floor (Sewer / Mines)** | **Procedural 3D Asteroid Belt**: Tumbling voxel or low-poly asteroids in microgravity. |
86: | **Bumper Reflex Arc** | **Radar Proximity & Harpoon Anchors**: Detecting asteroid surface approach and firing grapples. |
87: | **Refinery Crucible** | **Mothership Cargo Bay / Rocket Hopper**: Depositing raw space minerals (Platinum, Titanium, Dark Ice). |
88: | **Freight Elevator (-36m)** | **Deep Orbital Descent / Planetary Surface Landers**: Deploying bots from orbit to surface. |
89: | **Optical Sensor Blindness** | **Deep Space Solar Radiation / Nebula Storms**: Optical blind zones requiring sonar/radar pings. |
90: | **Robots (Flux, Tug, Scope, Dozer)** | **Specialized Drone Swarm**: <br>• **Harvester**: Drills & scoops ore<br>• **Carrier**: Transports mineral pods<br>• **Surveyor**: Scans asteroid density<br>• **Tug**: Steers asteroids into stable orbit |
91: | **Burn to Microchip** | **Autonomous Probe CPU**: Wire a guidance state machine, burn it to a flight computer chip, and install it on autonomous rockets! |
92:

94: 
95: ## 💡 4. Starter Blueprint for Space Miner Logic
96: 
97: When adapting the circuit engine for space mining, the sensor/actuator bus expands into orbital mechanics:
98: 
99: ```javascript
100: // Space Miner Sensor Bus
101: this.sensors = {
102:   proximity_front: false,  // Surface altimeter
103:   proximity_rear: false,
104:   ore_density_high: false, // Mineral vein scanner
105:   cargo_bay_full: false,   // Payload capacity sensor
106:   solar_intensity: false,  // Solar panel power level
107:   nav_beacon_ping: false,  // Mothership homing beacon
108:   gyro_stabilized: false   // Tumble / spin sensor
109: };
110: 
111: // Space Miner Actuator Bus
112: this.actuators = {
113:   rcs_thrust_forward: false, // Forward RCS impulse
114:   rcs_thrust_reverse: false, // Retro thruster braking
115:   rcs_yaw_left: false,       // Reaction wheel orientation
116:   rcs_yaw_right: false,
117:   harpoon_clamp: false,      // Anchor to asteroid crust
118:   plasma_drill: false,       // Surface mining beam
119:   docking_clamp: false       // Mothership lock
120: };
121: ```

124: 
125: ## 🛠️ 5. Deployment & Tech Stack Recipe
126: 
127: 1. **Runtime:** Vanilla JavaScript (ES Modules) — No React, Vue, or Angular overhead.
128: 2. **3D Library:** `three` (v0.170+)
129: 3. **Bundler & Dev Server:** `vite` (v6+) with Rollup production tree-shaking.
130: 4. **Target Output:** 4 files in `dist/` (`index.html`, `robots.txt`, `assets.js`, `assets.css`).
131: 5. **Hosting:** Any static Nginx, GitHub Pages, or VPS root.
132: 6. **Persistence:** `localStorage` JSON serialized memory card.

Hi Jack! Esperanza here. 

I love that idea! LogicForge's magic was that exact loop: **a live, tactile visual demo that pulls you in**, followed by **missions where learning a concept is the key that unlocks the next room, new abilities, and deeper gameplay.**

Applying that directly to learning **JavaScript** is a fantastic way to learn because every line of code immediately causes something to move, fire, scan, or unlock on screen.

---

### The Vision: **JS-FORGE (Cyber Drone Academy)**

A sci-fi training terminal built right here in [`JavaScriptTrainingCamp`](file:///home/jack/Desktop/JavaScriptTrainingCamp) featuring a split-screen station:

```
┌──────────────────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────┐
│          CYBER ARENA (Canvas)        │      THE CODE FORGE (Terminal)       │
│                                      │                                      │
│  [Grid Map with Neon Glow & Sparks]  │  • Mission Briefing & Concept Guide  │
│  [Animated Cyber Rover / Drone]      │  • In-Game JS Code Editor            │
│  [Laser Gates, Power Cores, Mines]   │  • [▶ RUN CODE]  [⏭ STEP]  [⏹ RESET]  │
│  [Real-Time Visual Line Highlighting]│  • Live Console Log & Diagnostics    │
│  [Procedural Web Audio FX]           │  • "Watch Demo" Auto-Play Mode       │
└──────────────────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────┘
```

---

### 1. The "LogicForge-Style" Learning Loop

1. **The Live Demo (Show, Don't Just Tell)**:
   - When you start or enter a new mission, you can click **"Watch Demo"**.
   - The rover springs to life, running a pre-built script: headlights turn on, thruster particles flare, and in the code editor, **each line of JavaScript lights up in real time** right as the drone executes it.
   - You hear procedural synth clicks, motor hums, and crystal collection chimes.

2. **The Progression Gate (Learn to Advance)**:
   - The demo hands the controls over to you. The puzzle layout shifts or introduces a new challenge.
   - You must write or modify real JavaScript in the editor to guide the drone to the extraction portal and collect energy cells.
   - When your code succeeds, the terminal plays an unlock fanfare, registers your achievement, and opens the next zone.

---

### 2. The Curriculum Roadmap

| Sector | JavaScript Concept | In-Game Mechanics & Challenge |
|---|---|---|
| **Sector 0: Demo Run** | *Code Execution & Syntax* | Watch the drone navigate a multi-obstacle course with live line-by-line code tracing. |
| **Sector 1: Vector Ignition** | `let`, `const`, Numbers, Function calls | Power up the drone, set `speed`, and navigate using `drone.forward()`, `drone.turnRight()`. |
| **Sector 2: Laser Interlocks** | `if`, `else if`, `else`, Booleans | Obstacles change colors or activate at random. Use `drone.scanAhead()` to avoid vaporizing shields. |
| **Sector 3: The Power Grid** | `for` and `while` Loops | A long corridor with 10 energy crystals. Instead of repeating 10 lines of code, forge an automated loop. |
| **Sector 4: Subroutines** | Custom Functions & Parameters | Package complex maneuvers into reusable functions (e.g. `disarmTrap(code)` or `collectRow(distance)`). |
| **Sector 5: Radar Array** | Arrays, `.length`, `.filter()`, `.forEach()` | The drone scans the room and receives an array of coordinates: `[{type: 'crystal', x: 2}, {type: 'mine', x: 4}]`. Filter out mines and navigate to targets. |
| **Sector 6: Drone Telemetry** | Objects, Properties & Methods | Read drone telemetry `{ battery: 85, shields: 100, cargo: [] }` and make dynamic decisions based on state. |
| **Sector 7: Cyber Arena** | Event-Driven Programming & Loops | Full sandbox with procedural mazes where your custom AI drone competes for high scores! |

---

### 3. Tech Stack (Clean, Zero-Bloat)

- **Engine**: Pure Vanilla JavaScript (ES Modules) + HTML5 Canvas.
- **Vite**: Instant dev-server reloading at 60 FPS.
- **Audio**: Procedural Web Audio API synthesizer (crisp retro-sci-fi blips, thruster rumbles, and chimes without any external MP3 files to download).
- **Editor**: Built-in lightweight in-browser editor with live stepping and safe sandbox execution.

---

### What to Try First:

1. **Watch the LogicForge-Style Demo**:

   - Watch the Cyber Rover fire up its thrusters, navigate the obstacles, bypass the laser firewall, and harvest the cores.

2. **Step-by-Step Mode**:
   - Hit **`↺ RESET BOT`**, then click **`⏭ STEP`** to advance one line of code at a time and inspect the telemetry.

3. **Take the Controls (Sector 1: Vector Ignition)**:
   - Use the sector dropdown in the top bar to switch to **Sector 1**.
   - Read the briefing, write your moves in the editor (`drone.move()`, `drone.turnRight()`), and click **`▶ RUN CODE`** to collect the energy cores and unlock the next zone!

+  async moveBackward(steps = 1) {
+    if (this.isDestroyed) {
+      this.logger.warn("Drone system destroyed. Reset required.");
+      return false;
+    }
+
+    const count = Math.max(1, Math.floor(steps));
+    for (let i = 0; i < count; i++) {
+      if (this.isDestroyed) return false;
+
+      // Report active line & await simulation tick if running via script runner
+      await this.runner.tick('moveBackward');
+
+      const vec = DIR_VECTORS[this.dir];
+      const targetX = this.x - vec.dx;
+      const targetY = this.y - vec.dy;
+      const key = `${targetX},${targetY}`;
+
+      // Check arena boundaries
+      if (targetX < 0 || targetX >= this.arena.gridWidth || targetY < 0 || targetY >= this.arena.gridHeight) {
+        sound.playError();
+        this.logger.error(`[COLLISION] Boundary breach at (${targetX}, ${targetY})! Path blocked.`);
+        return false;
+      }
+
+      // Check walls
+      if (this.arena.walls.has(key)) {
+        sound.playError();
+        this.logger.error(`[COLLISION] Wall impact at (${targetX}, ${targetY})!`);
+        return false;
+      }
+
+      // Check active lasers
+      if (this.arena.lasers.has(key)) {
+        const laser = this.arena.lasers.get(key);
+        if (laser.active !== false) {
+          sound.playLaserZap();
+          this.arena.triggerLaserZap(targetX, targetY);
+          this.shields -= 50;
+          this.logger.error(`[HAZARD] Laser beam struck! Shields at ${this.shields}%!`);
+          if (this.shields <= 0) {
+            this.isDestroyed = true;
+            this.logger.error(`[CRITICAL] Drone destroyed by laser firewall.`);
+            return false;
+          }
+        }
+      }
+
+      // Valid move
+      this.x = targetX;
+      this.y = targetY;
+      this.battery = Math.max(0, this.battery - 1);
+      sound.playStep();
+      this.arena.setDroneTarget(this.x, this.y, this.dir);
+
+      // Check crystals
+      if (this.arena.crystals.has(key)) {
+        this.arena.crystals.delete(key);
+        this.coresCollected++;
+        sound.playCollect();
+        this.arena.triggerCollect(this.x, this.y);
+        this.logger.info(`[HARVEST] Energy core acquired! (${this.coresCollected}/${this.totalCrystals})`);
+      }
+
+      // Check portal
+      if (this.arena.portal && this.x === this.arena.portal.x && this.y === this.arena.portal.y) {
+        this.reachedPortal = true;
+        this.logger.info(`[EXTRACTION] Portal zone reached!`);
+      }
+    }
+    return true;
+  }

Done, Jack! The manual controls are now fully wired up and ready to drive.

---

### 🕹️ What Was Fixed & Added:

1. **Unblocked the Simulation Tick**:
   - The engine was originally enforcing a script-runner pause before each step. We updated `runner.tick` and `CyberDrone` so that direct manual commands execute immediately with instant physics, audio, and particle effects.

2. **Expanded On-Screen D-Pad**:
   - **`▲`**: Drive Forward
   - **`◀`**: Turn 90° Left
   - **`▶`**: Turn 90° Right
   - **`▼`**: Drive Backward / Reverse
   - **`👁`**: Scan Ahead (reports what's 1 tile forward in the terminal)
   - **`⚡`**: Hack / Disarm (disables laser firewalls in front of you)

3. **Full Keyboard Controls**:
   - **`W`** / **`↑`**: Forward
   - **`S`** / **`↓`**: Reverse
   - **`A`** / **`←`**: Turn Left
   - **`D`** / **`→`**: Turn Right
   - **`Space`** or **`E`**: Scan Ahead
   - **`H`** or **`F`**: Hack Barrier
   - **`Escape`**: Instantly deselects the code editor so you can drive with keyboard without typing into the code box.

4. **Tactile Feedback & Telemetry**:
   - The on-screen D-Pad buttons now flash with a glowing cyan active state whenever you press their corresponding keys.
   - The top telemetry bar (`POS`, `HEADING`, `CORES`, `BATTERY`) updates instantly on every move.
   - If you manually drive through a sector, harvest the energy cores, and extract at the portal, the victory fanfare and sector clear modal will trigger just like it does for your scripts!

Head back to **[http://localhost:5173/](http://localhost:5173/)** (the changes have already hot-reloaded), click on the arena, and give the controls a spin!

the bot is drawn with javascript ?

<ADDITIONAL_METADATA>
The current local time is: 2026-10-01T09:26:14+08:00.
</ADDITIONAL_METADATA>
Yes, Jack! **100% pure JavaScript.** 

There are **zero image files, zero PNGs, zero sprites, and zero 3D models** in the entire project. 

Every single detail—from the angular cyber chassis and caterpillar treads to the glowing scanner headlights and exhaust sparks—is drawn dynamically on an HTML5 `<canvas>` using JavaScript math and vector drawing commands.

You can inspect the exact code right here in [`src/arena/canvas.js`](file:///home/jack/Desktop/JavaScriptTrainingCamp/src/arena/canvas.js#L388-L460).

---

### How JavaScript Draws the Bot:

#### 1. Rotating to Face the Drone's Direction
Instead of drawing 4 separate images for North, East, South, and West, JavaScript translates the canvas to the drone's coordinates `(x, y)` and rotates by `currentAngle`:

```javascript
ctx.save();
ctx.translate(px, py);
ctx.rotate(drone.currentAngle); // Smoothly turns when you turn!
```

#### 2. The Volumetric Headlight / Scanner Cone
A semi-transparent cyan radial gradient cast forward in an arc:

```javascript
const coneGrad = ctx.createRadialGradient(0, 0, 5, tileSize * 1.5, 0, tileSize * 2);
coneGrad.addColorStop(0, 'rgba(0, 240, 255, 0.35)');
coneGrad.addColorStop(1, 'rgba(0, 240, 255, 0.0)');

ctx.fillStyle = coneGrad;
ctx.beginPath();
ctx.moveTo(tileSize * 0.2, 0);
ctx.arc(0, 0, tileSize * 1.8, -0.42, 0.42); // Fan-shaped scanner beam
ctx.fill();
```

#### 3. The Cyber Hull & Tracks
Drawn with vector polygons and rectangles:

```javascript
// Tread pods (top and bottom tracks)
ctx.fillRect(-bodyW * 0.8, -bodyH - 4, bodyW * 1.6, 5);
ctx.fillRect(-bodyW * 0.8, bodyH - 1, bodyW * 1.6, 5);

// Chamfered cyber-chassis
ctx.beginPath();
ctx.moveTo(bodyW, 0);
ctx.lineTo(bodyW * 0.5, -bodyH);
ctx.lineTo(-bodyW * 0.7, -bodyH);
ctx.lineTo(-bodyW, 0);
ctx.lineTo(-bodyW * 0.7, bodyH);
ctx.lineTo(bodyW * 0.5, bodyH);
ctx.closePath();
ctx.fill();
```

#### 4. The Glowing Cyan Cockpit Core
JavaScript uses `shadowBlur` to create a real-time neon bloom without any Photoshop textures:

```javascript
ctx.fillStyle = '#00f0ff';
ctx.shadowColor = '#00f0ff';
ctx.shadowBlur = 8; // Neon cyber glow!
ctx.beginPath();
ctx.arc(bodyW * 0.15, 0, bodyW * 0.28, 0, Math.PI * 2);
ctx.fill();
```

#### 5. Pulsing Force Shield
Uses a sine wave based on the computer's clock (`performance.now()`) so the shield breathes:

```javascript
const shieldPulse = Math.sin(performance.now() * 0.005) * 2;
ctx.strokeStyle = 'rgba(0, 240, 255, 0.4)';
ctx.beginPath();
ctx.arc(0, 0, (tileSize * 0.44) + shieldPulse, 0, Math.PI * 2);
ctx.stroke();
```

#### 6. Dynamic Exhaust Particles
When you drive, JavaScript spawns particle objects into an array, calculating velocity with trigonometry (`Math.cos(backAngle) * speed`, `Math.sin(backAngle) * speed`), and fades their `alpha` each frame.

---

### The Power of Pure Code Graphics:
Because it’s drawn with code:
- **Instant load time**: The entire game is only ~48 KB total.
- **Scales cleanly**: Crisp on any display resolution or zoom level without pixelation.
- **Fully customizable**: We can change the drone's color, shape, armor, or weapons just by tweaking a few lines of JavaScript!

+  setupFabricatorUI() {
+    const btnOpen = document.getElementById('btnOpenDrawBot');
+    const btnClose = document.getElementById('btnCloseDrawBot');
+    const pixelGridEl = document.getElementById('pixelMatrixGrid');
+    const paletteEl = document.getElementById('paletteSwatches');
+    const chkSymmetry = document.getElementById('chkSymmetry');
+    const btnClear = document.getElementById('btnGridClear');
+    const btnSave = document.getElementById('btnSaveDrawBot');
+    const btnResetDefault = document.getElementById('btnResetBotDefault');
+    const previewCanvas = document.getElementById('botPreviewCanvas');
+    const previewCtx = previewCanvas.getContext('2d');
+
+    let currentColor = '#00f0ff';
+    let isDrawing = false;
+    let previewAngle = 0;
+    let previewAnimId = null;
+
+    // Open/Close Modal
+    const openModal = () => {
+      sound.init();
+      sound.playUiClick();
+      this.drawBotModal.classList.add('active');
+      renderGrid();
+      startPreview();
+    };
+
+    const closeModal = () => {
+      sound.playUiClick();
+      this.drawBotModal.classList.remove('active');
+      if (previewAnimId) {
+        cancelAnimationFrame(previewAnimId);
+        previewAnimId = null;
+      }
+    };
+
+    btnOpen?.addEventListener('click', openModal);
+    btnClose?.addEventListener('click', closeModal);
+
+    // 1. Render Palette Swatches
+    paletteEl.innerHTML = '';
+    COLOR_PALETTE.forEach(p => {
+      const swatch = document.createElement('div');
+      swatch.className = `palette-swatch ${p.hex === currentColor ? 'active-swatch' : ''}`;
+      if (p.hex === null) {
+        swatch.classList.add('eraser-swatch');
+        swatch.textContent = '✕';
+        swatch.title = 'Eraser (Clear Pixel)';
+      } else {
+        swatch.style.backgroundColor = p.hex;
+        swatch.title = `${p.name} (${p.hex})`;
+      }
+
+      swatch.addEventListener('click', () => {
+        sound.playUiClick();
+        document.querySelectorAll('.palette-swatch').forEach(s => s.classList.remove('active-swatch'));
+        swatch.classList.add('active-swatch');
+        currentColor = p.hex;
+      });
+
+      paletteEl.appendChild(swatch);
+    });
+
+    // 2. Render 16x16 Grid
+    const renderGrid = () => {
+      pixelGridEl.innerHTML = '';
+      for (let r = 0; r < GRID_SIZE; r++) {
+        for (let c = 0; c < GRID_SIZE; c++) {
+          const cell = document.createElement('div');
+          cell.className = 'pixel-cell';
+          cell.dataset.r = r;
+          cell.dataset.c = c;
+
+          const color = this.fabricator.getPixel(r, c);
+          if (color) {
+            cell.style.backgroundColor = color;
+          }
+
+          const paint = () => {
+            const sym = chkSymmetry.checked;
+            this.fabricator.setPixel(r, c, currentColor, sym);
+            cell.style.backgroundColor = currentColor || '';
+
+            if (sym) {
+              const symR = GRID_SIZE - 1 - r;
+              const symCell = pixelGridEl.querySelector(`[data-r="${symR}"][data-c="${c}"]`);
+              if (symCell) symCell.style.backgroundColor = currentColor || '';
+            }
+          };
+
+          cell.addEventListener('mousedown', (e) => {
+            e.preventDefault();
+            isDrawing = true;
+            paint();
+          });
+
+          cell.addEventListener('mouseenter', () => {
+            if (isDrawing) paint();
+          });
+
+          pixelGridEl.appendChild(cell);
+        }
+      }
+    };
+
+    window.addEventListener('mouseup', () => {
+      isDrawing = false;
+    });
+
+    // 3. Clear Matrix
+    btnClear?.addEventListener('click', () => {
+      sound.playUiClick();
+      this.fabricator.clear();
+      renderGrid();
+    });
+
+    // 4. Preset Buttons
+    document.querySelectorAll('.preset-btn').forEach(btn => {
+      btn.addEventListener('click', () => {
+        sound.playUiClick();
+        this.fabricator.loadPreset(btn.dataset.preset);
+        renderGrid();
+      });
+    });
+
+    // 5. Live Hologram Preview Animation
+    const startPreview = () => {
+      if (previewAnimId) cancelAnimationFrame(previewAnimId);
+
+      const loop = () => {
+        previewAngle += 0.02;
+
+        const w = previewCanvas.width;
+        const h = previewCanvas.height;
+        previewCtx.clearRect(0, 0, w, h);
+
+        // Circular hologram background
+        const grad = previewCtx.createRadialGradient(w/2, h/2, 10, w/2, h/2, 75);
+        grad.addColorStop(0, 'rgba(0, 240, 255, 0.15)');
+        grad.addColorStop(1, 'rgba(0, 0, 0, 0)');
+        previewCtx.fillStyle = grad;
+        previewCtx.beginPath();
+        previewCtx.arc(w/2, h/2, 75, 0, Math.PI * 2);
+        previewCtx.fill();
+
+        // Hologram grid rings
+        previewCtx.strokeStyle = 'rgba(0, 240, 255, 0.2)';
+        previewCtx.lineWidth = 1;
+        previewCtx.beginPath();
+        previewCtx.arc(w/2, h/2, 50, 0, Math.PI * 2);
+        previewCtx.arc(w/2, h/2, 70, 0, Math.PI * 2);
+        previewCtx.stroke();
+
+        // Render Bot in Center with current previewAngle
+        previewCtx.save();
+        previewCtx.translate(w/2, h/2);
+        previewCtx.rotate(previewAngle);
+
+        // Headlight beam
+        const cone = previewCtx.createRadialGradient(0, 0, 2, 70, 0, 80);
+        cone.addColorStop(0, 'rgba(0, 240, 255, 0.4)');
+        cone.addColorStop(1, 'rgba(0, 240, 255, 0)');
+        previewCtx.fillStyle = cone;
+        previewCtx.beginPath();
+        previewCtx.moveTo(10, 0);
+        previewCtx.arc(0, 0, 70, -0.4, 0.4);
+        previewCtx.closePath();
+        previewCtx.fill();
+
+        // Render custom bot pixels
+        const size = 64;
+        const pxSize = size / 16;
+        const startX = -size / 2;
+        const startY = -size / 2;
+
+        for (let r = 0; r < 16; r++) {
+          for (let c = 0; c < 16; c++) {
+            const col = this.fabricator.getPixel(r, c);
+            if (col) {
+              previewCtx.fillStyle = col;
+              previewCtx.fillRect(startX + c * pxSize, startY + r * pxSize, pxSize, pxSize);
+            }
+          }
+        }
+
+        // Shield ring
+        previewCtx.strokeStyle = 'rgba(0, 240, 255, 0.5)';
+        previewCtx.lineWidth = 1.5;
+        previewCtx.beginPath();
+        previewCtx.arc(0, 0, 36, 0, Math.PI * 2);
+        previewCtx.stroke();
+
+        previewCtx.restore();
+
+        previewAnimId = requestAnimationFrame(loop);
+      };
+      previewAnimId = requestAnimationFrame(loop);
+    };
+
+    // 6. Install on Drone
+    btnSave?.addEventListener('click', () => {
+      sound.playVictory();
+      this.fabricator.saveToStorage();
+      localStorage.removeItem('jsforge_custom_bot_disabled');
+      this.arena.setCustomBot(this.fabricator.grid);
+      this.logger.info(`[FABRICATOR] Custom drawn Cyber Rover installed on flight chassis!`);
+      closeModal();
+    });
+
+    // 7. Reset to Default Vector Chassis
+    btnResetDefault?.addEventListener('click', () => {
+      sound.playUiClick();
+      localStorage.setItem('jsforge_custom_bot_disabled', 'true');
+      this.arena.setCustomBot(null);
+      this.logger.info(`[FABRICATOR] Default procedural vector chassis restored.`);
+      closeModal();
+    });
+  }
 }
 
 // Boot application when DOM is ready
[diff_block_end]

### 🛠️ What Was Added:

1. **Top Nav Button**:

   - Click and drag across the 16x16 grid to draw your custom cyber rover.
   - **⇄ Vertical Symmetry Toggle**: Automatically mirrors your strokes top-to-bottom so designing symmetrical robots, mechs, and tanks is fast and satisfying.
   - Note: The **right side of the grid is the FRONT** (where the headlights point).

3. **Cyber Color Palette & Eraser**:
   - Neon Cyan, Plasma Pink, Cyber Yellow, Matrix Green, Electric Purple, Armor Blue, Dark Steel, Pure White, and an Eraser tool (`✕`).

     - **Classic Rover** (caterpillar treads + cyber hull)
     - **Stealth Scout** (needle-nose fighter drone)
     - **Siege Mech** (heavy armor plates + twin plasma cannons)

5. **Live Hologram Test Drive Preview**:
   - As you draw, a real-time hologram canvas on the right rotates your custom bot at 60 FPS inside a scanning laser grid, casting headlights and shield auras.

6. **⚡ Install On Drone**:
   - Click **`⚡ INSTALL ON DRONE`**, and your custom drawn bot is instantly deployed into the game arena!
   - When you drive with `W`, `A`, `S`, `D` or run your JavaScript scripts, **your custom drawn bot is what glides around the grid**, turns to face directions, casts headlight beams, and emits thruster spark trails!
   - Your custom robot is saved to `localStorage` so it stays with you across reloads.
   - Want the default vector rover back? Just click **`↺ DEFAULT VECTOR ROVER`** in the studio.

+  setupCodeBotUI() {
+    const btnOpen = document.getElementById('btnOpenCodeBot');
+    const btnClose = document.getElementById('btnCloseCodeBot');
+    const tabsContainer = document.getElementById('codeBotTabs');
+    const conceptBox = document.getElementById('codeBotConcept');
+    const textarea = document.getElementById('codeBotTextarea');
+    const consoleBox = document.getElementById('codeBotConsole');
+    const btnRun = document.getElementById('btnRunCodeBot');
+    const btnResetLesson = document.getElementById('btnResetCodeBotLesson');
+    const btnInstall = document.getElementById('btnInstallCodeBot');
+    const previewCanvas = document.getElementById('codeBotPreviewCanvas');
+    const previewCtx = previewCanvas.getContext('2d');
+
+    let currentLessonIndex = 0;
+    let compiledGrid = null;
+    let previewAngle = 0;
+    let previewAnimId = null;
+
+    const logToStudio = (type, msg) => {
+      const entry = document.createElement('div');
+      entry.className = `log-entry ${type}`;
+      const time = new Date().toLocaleTimeString().split(' ')[0];
+      entry.textContent = `[${time}] ${msg}`;
+      consoleBox.appendChild(entry);
+      consoleBox.scrollTop = consoleBox.scrollHeight;
+    };
+
+    const studioLogger = {
+      log: (...args) => logToStudio('log', args.join(' ')),
+      info: (...args) => logToStudio('info', args.join(' ')),
+      warn: (...args) => logToStudio('warn', args.join(' ')),
+      error: (...args) => logToStudio('error', args.join(' '))
+    };
+
+    // Open & Close Modal
+    const openModal = () => {
+      sound.init();
+      sound.playUiClick();
+      this.codeBotModal.classList.add('active');
+      loadLesson(currentLessonIndex);
+      startPreview();
+    };
+
+    const closeModal = () => {
+      sound.playUiClick();
+      this.codeBotModal.classList.remove('active');
+      if (previewAnimId) {
+        cancelAnimationFrame(previewAnimId);
+        previewAnimId = null;
+      }
+    };
+
+    btnOpen?.addEventListener('click', openModal);
+    btnClose?.addEventListener('click', closeModal);
+
+    // Render Lesson Tabs
+    tabsContainer.innerHTML = '';
+    CODE_BOT_LESSONS.forEach((lesson, idx) => {
+      const tabBtn = document.createElement('button');
+      tabBtn.className = `codebot-tab-btn ${idx === currentLessonIndex ? 'active-tab' : ''}`;
+      tabBtn.textContent = lesson.title;
+      tabBtn.addEventListener('click', () => {
+        sound.playUiClick();
+        document.querySelectorAll('.codebot-tab-btn').forEach(b => b.classList.remove('active-tab'));
+        tabBtn.classList.add('active-tab');
+        loadLesson(idx);
+      });
+      tabsContainer.appendChild(tabBtn);
+    });
+
+    const loadLesson = (idx) => {
+      currentLessonIndex = idx;
+      const lesson = CODE_BOT_LESSONS[idx];
+      conceptBox.innerHTML = `<strong>${lesson.conceptTitle}</strong><br>${lesson.conceptSummary || lesson.conceptExplanation}`;
+      textarea.value = lesson.starterCode;
+      compileCode();
+    };
+
+    // Compile Code from Textarea
+    const compileCode = () => {
+      const code = textarea.value;
+      const result = this.botCompiler.compile(code, studioLogger);
+      if (result.success) {
+        compiledGrid = result.grid;
+        sound.playStep();
+        logToStudio('info', `Compilation successful! Grid matrix populated.`);
+      } else {
+        sound.playError();
+        logToStudio('error', `Build failed: ${result.error?.message}`);
+      }
+    };
+
+    btnRun?.addEventListener('click', () => {
+      sound.init();
+      compileCode();
+    });
+
+    btnResetLesson?.addEventListener('click', () => {
+      sound.playUiClick();
+      loadLesson(currentLessonIndex);
+      logToStudio('info', 'Lesson starter code restored.');
+    });
+
+    // Install on Drone
+    btnInstall?.addEventListener('click', () => {
+      compileCode();
+      if (compiledGrid) {
+        sound.playVictory();
+        this.fabricator.grid = compiledGrid;
+        this.fabricator.saveToStorage();
+        localStorage.removeItem('jsforge_custom_bot_disabled');
+        this.arena.setCustomBot(compiledGrid);
+        this.logger.info(`[CODE BOT] Code-generated robot deployed to arena flight computer!`);
+        closeModal();
+      }
+    });
+
+    // 60 FPS Hologram Preview Loop
+    const startPreview = () => {
+      if (previewAnimId) cancelAnimationFrame(previewAnimId);
+
+      const loop = () => {
+        previewAngle += 0.02;
+
+        const w = previewCanvas.width;
+        const h = previewCanvas.height;
+        previewCtx.clearRect(0, 0, w, h);
+
+        // Circular cyber radar background
+        const grad = previewCtx.createRadialGradient(w/2, h/2, 10, w/2, h/2, 85);
+        grad.addColorStop(0, 'rgba(0, 255, 136, 0.15)');
+        grad.addColorStop(1, 'rgba(0, 0, 0, 0)');
+        previewCtx.fillStyle = grad;
+        previewCtx.beginPath();
+        previewCtx.arc(w/2, h/2, 85, 0, Math.PI * 2);
+        previewCtx.fill();
+
+        // Hologram grid rings
+        previewCtx.strokeStyle = 'rgba(0, 255, 136, 0.25)';
+        previewCtx.lineWidth = 1;
+        previewCtx.beginPath();
+        previewCtx.arc(w/2, h/2, 55, 0, Math.PI * 2);
+        previewCtx.arc(w/2, h/2, 80, 0, Math.PI * 2);
+        previewCtx.stroke();
+
+        // Center crosshair
+        previewCtx.strokeStyle = 'rgba(0, 255, 136, 0.15)';
+        previewCtx.beginPath();
+        previewCtx.moveTo(w/2 - 80, h/2);
+        previewCtx.lineTo(w/2 + 80, h/2);
+        previewCtx.moveTo(w/2, h/2 - 80);
+        previewCtx.lineTo(w/2, h/2 + 80);
+        previewCtx.stroke();
+
+        // Render Bot in Center
+        previewCtx.save();
+        previewCtx.translate(w/2, h/2);
+        previewCtx.rotate(previewAngle);
+
+        // Forward Scanner Beam
+        const cone = previewCtx.createRadialGradient(0, 0, 2, 75, 0, 85);
+        cone.addColorStop(0, 'rgba(0, 255, 136, 0.4)');
+        cone.addColorStop(1, 'rgba(0, 255, 136, 0)');
+        previewCtx.fillStyle = cone;
+        previewCtx.beginPath();
+        previewCtx.moveTo(10, 0);
+        previewCtx.arc(0, 0, 75, -0.4, 0.4);
+        previewCtx.closePath();
+        previewCtx.fill();
+
+        // Render compiled grid pixels
+        if (compiledGrid) {
+          const size = 76;
+          const pxSize = size / 16;
+          const startX = -size / 2;
+          const startY = -size / 2;
+
+          for (let r = 0; r < 16; r++) {
+            for (let c = 0; c < 16; c++) {
+              const col = compiledGrid[r][c];
+              if (col) {
+                previewCtx.fillStyle = col;
+                previewCtx.fillRect(startX + c * pxSize, startY + r * pxSize, pxSize, pxSize);
+              }
+            }
+          }
+        }
+
+        // Shield perimeter
+        previewCtx.strokeStyle = 'rgba(0, 255, 136, 0.5)';
+        previewCtx.lineWidth = 1.5;
+        previewCtx.beginPath();
+        previewCtx.arc(0, 0, 44, 0, Math.PI * 2);
+        previewCtx.stroke();
+
+        previewCtx.restore();
+
+        previewAnimId = requestAnimationFrame(loop);
+      };
+      previewAnimId = requestAnimationFrame(loop);
+    };
+
+    // Tab key handling in codebot-textarea (indent 2 spaces)
+    textarea.addEventListener('keydown', (e) => {
+      if (e.key === 'Tab') {
+        e.preventDefault();
+        const start = textarea.selectionStart;
+        const end = textarea.selectionEnd;
+        const val = textarea.value;
+        textarea.value = val.substring(0, start) + '  ' + val.substring(end);
+        textarea.selectionStart = textarea.selectionEnd = start + 2;
+      }
+    });
+  }

│ │   grid[1][c] = colors.DARK; // Treads     │ │                               │

└───────────────────────────────────────────────┴───────────────────────────────┘
                     [⚡ INSTALL ON ARENA DRONE]
```

---

### The Three JavaScript Lessons:

#### 1. Lesson 1: Coordinates & 2D Arrays (`grid[r][c]`)
- **The Concept**: In graphics, every sprite is a grid of rows and columns (0 to 15).
- **The Code**:
  ```javascript
  // Paint the glowing cockpit core in the center:
  grid[7][7] = colors.CYAN;
  grid[7][8] = colors.CYAN;
  grid[8][7] = colors.CYAN;
  grid[8][8] = colors.CYAN;

  // Add front twin laser cannons (Column 14 is near the front):
  grid[6][14] = colors.YELLOW;
  grid[9][14] = colors.YELLOW;
  ```
- **What You Learn**: Array indexing, variables, and coordinate spaces.

#### 2. Lesson 2: Automating Armor & Treads with `for` Loops
- **The Concept**: Why type 20 coordinates by hand when a loop can do it in 3 lines?
- **The Code**:
  ```javascript
  // Draw an entire 12-pixel tank caterpillar track using a loop:
  for (let c = 2; c <= 13; c++) {
    grid[1][c] = colors.DARK;   // Outer tread
    grid[2][c] = colors.ARMOR;  // Inner steel plate
  }

  // Nested loop to fill the armored central chassis hull:
  for (let r = 5; r <= 10; r++) {
    for (let c = 3; c <= 12; c++) {
      grid[r][c] = colors.DARK;
    }
  }
  ```
- **What You Learn**: `for` loops, loop counters, and 2D nested loops.

#### 3. Lesson 3: Angled Stealth Wings & Geometry (`if` + Math)
- **The Concept**: Game engines use mathematical formulas to calculate angled lines and aerodynamic wings.
- **The Code**:
  ```javascript
  // Calculate sweeping angled wings based on column distance:
  for (let col = 3; col <= 13; col++) {
    let spread = Math.floor((col - 3) * 0.55);
    grid[7 - spread][col] = colors.PURPLE; // Sweeps upward
    grid[8 + spread][col] = colors.PURPLE; // Sweeps downward
  }
  ```
- **What You Learn**: Math calculations (`Math.floor()`), dynamic geometry, and symmetrical offsets.

---

### ⚡ Take It for a Test Drive:
1. Edit any number or color in the code box (e.g. change `colors.CYAN` to `colors.PINK`, or make the tracks longer).
2. Click **`▶ COMPILE & RUN`** to immediately see your code generate the new bot in the live 3D/2D hologram preview.
3. Click **`⚡ INSTALL ON ARENA DRONE`**!
4. Close the window and hit **`W`**, **`A`**, **`S`**, **`D`**—**the bot you programmed with JavaScript is now driving around the arena!**

1: # ⚡ LOGICFORGE-V2 2026: ENGINEERING LAB MANUAL & GATE THEORY
2: 
3: > *"In 1854, English mathematician George Boole published 'An Investigation of the Laws of Thought', demonstrating that logical propositions could be reduced to two binary states: TRUE (1) and FALSE (0). Every digital computer on Earth—from the Apollo Guidance Computer to modern supercomputers—is built from these exact foundational logic gates."*
4: 
5: ---
6: 
7: ## 📖 TABLE OF CONTENTS
8: 1. [Digital Logic Gate Theory & Truth Tables](#1-digital-logic-gate-theory--truth-tables)
9: 2. [Flux's Motherboard Architecture & Pinout](#2-fluxs-motherboard-architecture--pinout)
10: 3. [Robot Controls & Piloting Manual](#3-robot-controls--piloting-manual)
11: 4. [Carriable Items & Grabber Mechanics](#4-carriable-items--grabber-mechanics)
12: 5. [The "Burn to Microchip" Innovation Lab](#5-the-burn-to-microchip-innovation-lab)
13: 6. [The 8 Educational Curriculum Missions](#6-the-8-educational-curriculum-missions)
14: 
15: ---
16: 
17: ## 1. DIGITAL LOGIC GATE THEORY & TRUTH TABLES
18: 
19: In digital electronics, voltages are abstracted as binary states:
20: - **HIGH (1)** = Active / 5V / True
21: - **LOW (0)** = Inactive / 0V / False
22: 
23: ### 1.1 AND Gate (7408 TTL)
24: - **Boolean Equation:** $Y = A \cdot B$ *(Conjunction)*
25: - **Rule:** The output is **1** if and only if **BOTH** Input A and Input B are **1**. If either input is 0, the output is 0.
26: - **Robotics Use:** Safety interlocks and authorization circuits (e.g. Proximity Radar AND Clock pulse to open an airlock).
27: 
28: | Input A | Input B | Output (Y) |
29: | :---: | :---: | :---: |
30: | 0 | 0 | **0** |
31: | 0 | 1 | **0** |
32: | 1 | 0 | **0** |
33: | 1 | 1 | **1** |
34: 
35: ---
36: 
37: ### 1.2 OR Gate (7432 TTL)
38: - **Boolean Equation:** $Y = A + B$ *(Disjunction)*
39: - **Rule:** The output is **1** if **EITHER** Input A or Input B (or both) is **1**. The output is 0 only when both inputs are 0.
40: - **Robotics Use:** Command arbitration (e.g. fire thrusters if AI Navigation requests it OR if an Emergency Bumper requests it).
41: 
42: | Input A | Input B | Output (Y) |
43: | :---: | :---: | :---: |
44: | 0 | 0 | **0** |
45: | 0 | 1 | **1** |
46: | 1 | 0 | **1** |
47: | 1 | 1 | **1** |
48: 
49: ---
50: 
51: ### 1.3 NOT Inverter (7404 TTL)
52: - **Boolean Equation:** $Y = \overline{A}$ *(Negation)*
53: - **Rule:** Inverts the incoming digital signal: converts 0 into 1, and 1 into 0.
54: - **Robotics Use:** Active-low sensor logic; reversing thruster direction upon collision (e.g. striking the North wall triggers South thrust).
55: 
56: | Input A | Output (Y) |
57: | :---: | :---: |
58: | 0 | **1** |
59: | 1 | **0** |
60: 
61: ---
62: 
63: ### 1.4 XOR Gate (7486 TTL — Exclusive-OR)
64: - **Boolean Equation:** $Y = A \oplus B$ *(Exclusive Disjunction)*
65: - **Rule:** The output is **1** if the inputs are **DIFFERENT** (one is 1, the other is 0). The output is 0 if both inputs are identical.
66: - **Robotics Use:** Dual-airlock safety interlocks (passage permitted only when exactly one hatch is open), parity checking, and binary half-adders.
67: 
68: | Input A | Input B | Output (Y) |
69: | :---: | :---: | :---: |
70: | 0 | 0 | **0** |
71: | 0 | 1 | **1** |
72: | 1 | 0 | **1** |
73: | 1 | 1 | **0** |
74: 
75: ---
76: 
77: ### 1.5 NAND Gate (7400 TTL — Universal Gate)
78: - **Boolean Equation:** $Y = \overline{A \cdot B}$ *(Inverted AND)*
79: - **Rule:** The output is **0** only when both inputs are **1**. In all other states, the output is **1**.
80: - **Robotics Use:** Universal logic building block. In computer hardware engineering, any logic circuit (including full CPUs) can be constructed exclusively using NAND gates.
81: 
82: | Input A | Input B | Output (Y) |
83: | :---: | :---: | :---: |
84: | 0 | 0 | **1** |
85: | 0 | 1 | **1** |
86: | 1 | 0 | **1** |
87: | 1 | 1 | **0** |
88: 
89: ---
90: 
91: ### 1.6 NOR Gate (7402 TTL — Universal Gate)
92: - **Boolean Equation:** $Y = \overline{A + B}$ *(Inverted OR)*
93: - **Rule:** The output is **1** only when both inputs are **0**. If any input is 1, the output is **0**.
94: - **Robotics Use:** Universal logic building block; reset-priority safety latches and lockout circuits.
95: 
96: | Input A | Input B | Output (Y) |
97: | :---: | :---: | :---: |
98: | 0 | 0 | **1** |
99: | 0 | 1 | **0** |
100: | 1 | 0 | **0** |

+  setupManualModal() {
+    const btnOpen = document.getElementById('btnOpenManual');
+    const btnClose = document.getElementById('btnCloseManual');
+    const btnCloseFooter = document.getElementById('btnCloseManualFooter');
+    const tocContainer = document.getElementById('manualToc');
+    const contentBox = document.getElementById('manualContent');
+
+    let currentChapterIndex = 0;
+
+    const openModal = () => {
+      sound.init();
+      sound.playUiClick();
+      this.manualModal.classList.add('active');
+      renderToc();
+      loadChapter(currentChapterIndex);
+    };
+
+    const closeModal = () => {
+      sound.playUiClick();
+      this.manualModal.classList.remove('active');
+    };
+
+    btnOpen?.addEventListener('click', openModal);
+    btnClose?.addEventListener('click', closeModal);
+    btnCloseFooter?.addEventListener('click', closeModal);
+
+    const renderToc = () => {
+      tocContainer.innerHTML = '';
+      MANUAL_CHAPTERS.forEach((chap, idx) => {
+        const btn = document.createElement('button');
+        btn.className = `manual-toc-btn ${idx === currentChapterIndex ? 'active-toc' : ''}`;
+        btn.innerHTML = `<span class="toc-num">#${chap.num}</span><span>${chap.title}</span>`;
+        btn.addEventListener('click', () => {
+          sound.playUiClick();
+          document.querySelectorAll('.manual-toc-btn').forEach(b => b.classList.remove('active-toc'));
+          btn.classList.add('active-toc');
+          loadChapter(idx);
+        });
+        tocContainer.appendChild(btn);
+      });
+    };
+
+    const loadChapter = (idx) => {
+      currentChapterIndex = idx;
+      const chap = MANUAL_CHAPTERS[idx];
+      contentBox.innerHTML = chap.content;
+      contentBox.scrollTop = 0;
+    };
+  }
 }

---

### 📚 The 8 Chapters in the Manual:

| Chapter | Topic | What It Covers |
| :---: | :--- | :--- |
| **#1** | **Execution Engine & JS Theory** | Sequential execution, program counter, and physical actuator timing. |
| **#2** | **Variables, Types & Memory** | `let` vs `const`, immutability, numbers, strings, and booleans. |
| **#3** | **Conditionals & Logic Interlocks** | `===`, `!==`, logic gates (`&&`, `\|\|`, `!`), and `if / else` branching. |
| **#4** | **Loops & Iteration Automation** | `for` loops, `while` loops, the DRY principle, and infinite loop watchdogs. |
| **#5** | **Functions & Subroutines** | Packaging robot maneuvers into reusable subroutines with arguments. |
| **#6** | **Data Structures (Arrays & Objects)** | Target arrays, `.filter()`, `.forEach()`, and `{ battery, shields }` telemetry dictionaries. |
| **#7** | **CyberDrone Hardware API** | Complete reference for all navigation, sensor, radar, and hack commands. |
| **#8** | **Graphics Programming Theory** | 2D coordinate matrices, pixel generation, and Canvas 2D vector drawing. |

### 1. Task Overview
- **User**: Jack (assistant name: Esperanza).
- **Goal**: Create an interactive game to learn JavaScript inspired directly by Jack's previous project **`LOGICFORGE-V2`** (a living visual demo + curriculum where learning logic/code is required to unlock abilities and advance).
- **Recent Directive**: Keep everything already built intact (Demo, Missions, Manual D-Pad, Draw Bot, Code A Bot, Manual & Theory) as "DEMO material", but add a **`🚀 START GAME`** (Campaign Mode) mirroring LogicForge:
  - When you actually start the game, you begin at ground zero: **no bot on the field** and **no propulsion**.
  - You have tasks, but you must **build the bot** in the fabricator and **code the controls** (forward engine, gyro steering, optical sensors, automation loops) to accomplish your tasks.

---

### 2. Progress
- **Project Location**: `/home/jack/Desktop/JavaScriptTrainingCamp`
- **Active Server**: Vite v8.3.1 running locally at `http://localhost:5173/` (Task ID: `task-48`).
- **Completed Components**:
  - `index.html` & `style.css`: Cyberpunk split-screen layout (Canvas arena on left, Code Forge on right). Added `🚀 START GAME` toggle button in header and `#hardwareRibbon` HUD (`CHASSIS`, `PROPULSION`, `GYRO STEERING`, `OPTICAL SENSOR`, `AUTOMATION LOOP`).
  - `src/audio/synth.js`: 100% procedural Web Audio API synthesizer (clicks, thrusters, turns, pings, crystal chimes, victory fanfare, error buzzer).
  - `src/arena/canvas.js`: 60 FPS HTML5 Canvas renderer with smooth interpolation, lighting, particle sparks, custom bot matrix rendering, and `isCradleMode` (renders empty glowing assembly cradle when no robot is installed).
  - `src/arena/customBot.js`: 16x16 pixel matrix fabricator with symmetry drawing, color palette, presets (Rover, Scout, Tank), and `localStorage` persistence.
  - `src/engine/botCompiler.js`: In-browser compiler for programming robot sprites using real JavaScript code (2D arrays, `for` loops, math).
  - `src/engine/runner.js`: Safe execution sandbox with step-by-step mode, line highlighting, and infinite loop watchdog.
  - `src/engine/drone.js`: Virtual hardware API (`move`, `moveBackward`, `turnRight`, `turnLeft`, `scanAhead`, `scan`, `radar`, `hack`). Updated with `setPermissionCheck(fn)` for campaign stage lockouts.
  - `src/missions/curriculum.js`: Sectors 0 to 5 for Sandbox/Demo mode.

622:       if (p.hex === null) {
623:         swatch.classList.add('eraser-swatch');
624:         swatch.textContent = '✕';

629:       }

641:     // 2. Render 16x16 Grid
642:     const renderGrid = () => {
643:       pixelGridEl.innerHTML = '';
644:       for (let r = 0; r < GRID_SIZE; r++) {
645:         for (let c = 0; c < GRID_SIZE; c++) {
646:           const cell = document.createElement('div');
647:           cell.className = 'pixel-cell';
648:           cell.dataset.r = r;
649:           cell.dataset.c = c;
650: 
651:           const color = this.fabricator.getPixel(r, c);
652:           if (color) {
653:             cell.style.backgroundColor = color;
654:           }
655: 
656:           const paint = () => {
657:             const sym = chkSymmetry.checked;
658:             this.fabricator.setPixel(r, c, currentColor, sym);
659:             cell.style.backgroundColor = currentColor || '';
660: 
661:             if (sym) {
662:               const symR = GRID_SIZE - 1 - r;
663:               const symCell = pixelGridEl.querySelector(`[data-r="${symR}"][data-c="${c}"]`);
664:               if (symCell) symCell.style.backgroundColor = currentColor || '';
665:             }
666:           };
667: 
668:           cell.addEventListener('mousedown', (e) => {
669:             e.preventDefault();
670:             isDrawing = true;
671:             paint();
672:           });

238: export class CampaignManager {
239:   constructor() {
240:     this.isActive = false;
241:     this.currentStageIndex = 0;
242:     this.hasBuiltChassis = false;
243:     this.loadState();
244:   }
245: 
246:   loadState() {
247:     try {
248:       const active = localStorage.getItem('jsforge_campaign_active');
249:       if (active !== null) {
250:         this.isActive = active === 'true';
251:       }
252:       const stage = localStorage.getItem('jsforge_campaign_stage');
253:       if (stage !== null) {
254:         this.currentStageIndex = parseInt(stage, 10) || 0;
255:       }
256:       const built = localStorage.getItem('jsforge_custom_bot_installed');
257:       if (built !== null) {
258:         this.hasBuiltChassis = built === 'true';
259:       }
260:     } catch (e) {
261:       console.warn("Storage restricted", e);
262:     }
263:   }
264: 
265:   saveState() {
266:     try {
267:       localStorage.setItem('jsforge_campaign_active', this.isActive);
268:       localStorage.setItem('jsforge_campaign_stage', this.currentStageIndex);
269:       localStorage.setItem('jsforge_custom_bot_installed', this.hasBuiltChassis);
270:     } catch (e) {
271:       // Ignore
272:     }
273:   }
274: 
275:   startNewGame() {
276:     this.isActive = true;
277:     this.currentStageIndex = 0;
278:     this.hasBuiltChassis = false;
279:     this.saveState();
280:   }
281: 
282:   exitToDemo() {
283:     this.isActive = false;
284:     this.saveState();
285:   }
286: 
287:   getCurrentStage() {
288:     return CAMPAIGN_STAGES[this.currentStageIndex] || CAMPAIGN_STAGES[0];
289:   }
290: 
291:   markChassisBuilt() {
292:     this.hasBuiltChassis = true;
293:     this.saveState();
294:   }
295: 
296:   advanceStage() {
297:     if (this.currentStageIndex < CAMPAIGN_STAGES.length - 1) {
298:       this.currentStageIndex++;
299:       this.saveState();
300:       return true;
301:     }
302:     return false;
303:   }
304: }
305:

   setupUI() {
+    // Mode Toggle (START GAME vs EXIT TO DEMO)
+    this.btnToggleGameMode?.addEventListener('click', () => {
+      sound.playUiClick();
+      if (this.campaign.isActive) {
+        // Exit to Demo
+        this.campaign.exitToDemo();
+        this.btnToggleGameMode.textContent = '🚀 START GAME';
+        this.btnToggleGameMode.classList.add('btn-run');
+        this.btnToggleGameMode.classList.remove('btn-demo');
+        this.hardwareRibbon.style.display = 'none';
+        this.arena.setCradleMode(false);
+        this.loadSector(this.currentSectorIndex);
+        this.logger.info("[MODE] Returned to Sandbox & Demo Curriculum.");
+      } else {
+        // Start Game (Campaign)
+        this.campaign.isActive = true;
+        this.campaign.saveState();
+        this.btnToggleGameMode.textContent = '📺 EXIT TO DEMO';
+        this.btnToggleGameMode.classList.remove('btn-run');
+        this.btnToggleGameMode.classList.add('btn-demo');

Hey Jack! It’s **Esperanza**.

I have implemented the **`🚀 START GAME` Campaign Mode**, directly mirroring the core progression philosophy of **`LOGICFORGE`**!

All existing demo material, manual controls, curriculum sectors, Draw Bot, Code A Bot, and Theory Manual remain 100% intact as the **Demo Sandbox**. Now, clicking **`🚀 START GAME`** boots you into a true **ground-zero campaign**:

---

### 🕹️ What Happens When You Click `🚀 START GAME`

```
[ DEMO SANDBOX ]  <──────>  [ 🚀 START GAME (CAMPAIGN) ]
(All tools unlocked)         (Ground Zero: No Bot, No Propulsion)
```

1. **Empty Assembly Cradle (No Bot On Field)**:
   - When you boot the game, the arena renders an empty, glowing yellow assembly cradle with `NO BOT / CRADLE EMPTY`.
   - The new **Hardware Firmware Ribbon** at the top of the arena shows your subsystems:
     - ❌ **CHASSIS**
     - ❌ **PROPULSION**
     - ❌ **GYRO STEERING**
     - ❌ **OPTICAL SENSOR**
     - ❌ **AUTOMATION LOOP**
   - If you attempt to run code or press the manual D-pad before building, the flight computer buzzes with:  
     `[HARDWARE ERROR] No robot detected on assembly cradle! Use DRAW BOT or CODE A BOT to construct your chassis first.`

2. **Stage 1: Fabrication Protocol (Build the Bot)**:
   - **Task**: Fabricate your machine using either [🎨 DRAW BOT](file:///home/jack/Desktop/JavaScriptTrainingCamp/index.html) (matrix pixel artist) or [💻 CODE A BOT](file:///home/jack/Desktop/JavaScriptTrainingCamp/index.html) (procedural JavaScript).
   - Once you click **"INSTALL"**, the bot drops onto the cradle pad, the `CHASSIS` badge lights up neon green (✅ **CHASSIS**), fanfare sounds, and Stage 1 completes!

3. **Stage 2: Vector Ignition (Code Forward Propulsion)**:
   - **Task**: The robot is physically on the pad, but firmware has no propulsion code.
   - Steering and sensors are hardware-locked. You must write and run [`drone.move(3)`](file:///home/jack/Desktop/JavaScriptTrainingCamp/src/engine/campaign.js#L80-L94) to fire the caterpillar tracks and harvest the Spark Plug Core.
   - Completing this unlocks ✅ **PROPULSION**.

4. **Stage 3: Gyro Steering Servos (Code Rotational Steering)**:
   - **Task**: The industrial corridor makes an L-bend. Driving straight will crash into steel bulkheads.
   - You must program the servo rotation using [`drone.turnRight()`](file:///home/jack/Desktop/JavaScriptTrainingCamp/src/engine/campaign.js#L123-L141) in tandem with forward thrust to reach the docking port.
   - Completing this unlocks ✅ **GYRO STEERING**.

5. **Stage 4: Optical Scanner & Laser Interlock (Obstacle Sensing & Cyber Warfare)**:
   - **Task**: An electrified laser barrier blocks the airlock. If you drive forward blindly, the bot is destroyed.
   - You must code the optical sensor with [`let item = drone.scanAhead()`](file:///home/jack/Desktop/JavaScriptTrainingCamp/src/engine/campaign.js#L171-L196) and write an `if` statement to trigger [`drone.hack()`](file:///home/jack/Desktop/JavaScriptTrainingCamp/src/engine/drone.js) to drop the firewall.
   - Completing this unlocks ✅ **OPTICAL SENSOR**.

6. **Stage 5: Automation Co-Processor (Code Loops to Survive Battery Drain)**:
   - **Task**: Six energy cores are lined up in the conduit, but your auxiliary battery is draining.
   - You must code a JavaScript `for` or `while` loop to harvest all 6 cores efficiently.
   - Completing this unlocks ✅ **AUTOMATION LOOP**, fully evolving your machine from zero into an autonomous cyber rover!

7. **Seamless Toggle Between Modes**:
   - The top header button dynamically switches between `🚀 START GAME` and `📺 EXIT TO DEMO`. You can test concepts in Demo mode anytime, then return right to your campaign progress.

---

**Yes, 100% absolutely, Jack!**

In fact, it is **exceptionally easy and lightweight** to host on any Linux VPS (even a tiny $3/month or free-tier instance).

---

### 💡 Why It Runs Effortlessly on Your VPS

1. **Zero Heavy Backend Requirements**:
   - There is **no database** (Postgres, Mongo, etc.) to configure.
   - There is **no backend runtime** needed for the game logic.
2. **Client-Side Computing**:
   - The 60 FPS HTML5 Canvas renderer, procedural Web Audio sound synthesizer, and sandboxed code execution runner **run 100% in the visitor's browser**.
3. **Virtually Zero Server Resource Usage**:
   - Your VPS only serves static files (`HTML`, `CSS`, `JS`). Serving this takes **less than 15 MB of RAM** and practically 0% CPU on your server!

---

### 🛠️ 2 Simple Ways to Run It on Your VPS

#### Option 1: Nginx or Caddy (Recommended — Fastest & Lightest)
Build the production bundle and copy the [`dist/`](file:///home/jack/Desktop/JavaScriptTrainingCamp/dist) folder:

```bash
# 1. On your machine (or VPS):
npm run build

# 2. Copy the dist folder to your VPS web root:
# e.g. /var/www/javascript-training-camp
```

**Quick Nginx Configuration (`/etc/nginx/sites-available/jsforge`):**
```nginx
server {
    listen 80;
    server_name your-domain-or-ip;

    root /var/www/javascript-training-camp/dist;
    index index.html;

    location / {
        try_files $uri $uri/ /index.html;
    }
}
```

---

#### Option 2: Node.js / PM2 / Vite Preview
If you prefer running a Node process with PM2 or systemd:

```bash
# In the project directory on your VPS:
npm run build

# Serve production build on port 3000 (accessible publicly):
npm run preview -- --host 0.0.0.0 --port 3000
```

Or using the standard lightweight `serve` package:
```bash
npx serve -s dist -l 3000
```

---

Whenever you want to push it up or need a Dockerfile / Nginx conf customized for your domain, just let me know!

I've created the exact Nginx configuration named **`js_forge`**, mirroring your **`logicforge`** setup on your VPS down to the exact directories, SSL certbot flags, and gzip compression.

I've also saved it directly in your project under [`deploy/js_forge`](file:///home/jack/Desktop/JavaScriptTrainingCamp/deploy/js_forge) along with a deployment helper script [`deploy/deploy_to_vps.sh`](file:///home/jack/Desktop/JavaScriptTrainingCamp/deploy/deploy_to_vps.sh).

---

### 📄 The Nginx Configuration: `/etc/nginx/sites-available/js_forge`

```nginx
# ==============================================================================
# NGINX CONFIGURATION: js_forge
# File Location: /etc/nginx/sites-available/js_forge
# Symlink: ln -sfn /etc/nginx/sites-available/js_forge /etc/nginx/sites-enabled/js_forge
# ==============================================================================

# HTTP -> Redirect all traffic to HTTPS
server {
    listen 80;
    listen [::]:80;
    server_name jsforge.staacornews.com js-forge.staacornews.com;

    # Let's Encrypt challenge path
    location /.well-known/acme-challenge/ {
        root /var/www/js_forge;
    }

    location / {
        return 301 https://$host$request_uri;
    }
}

# HTTPS -> Production Cyber Arena Application
server {
    listen 443 ssl;
    listen [::]:443 ssl;
    server_name jsforge.staacornews.com js-forge.staacornews.com;

    root /var/www/js_forge;
    index index.html;

    # SSL Certificates (managed via Certbot)
    ssl_certificate /etc/letsencrypt/live/jsforge.staacornews.com/fullchain.pem;
    ssl_certificate_key /etc/letsencrypt/live/jsforge.staacornews.com/privkey.pem;
    include /etc/letsencrypt/options-ssl-nginx.conf;
    ssl_dhparam /etc/letsencrypt/ssl-dhparams.pem;

    # Application Routing & Fallback
    location / {
        try_files $uri $uri/ /index.html;
    }

    # High-performance asset caching (Vite hashed bundles)
    location /assets/ {
        expires 1y;
        add_header Cache-Control "public, immutable";
    }

    # Gzip Compression (Optimized for JS code & audio web data)
    gzip on;
    gzip_vary on;
    gzip_proxied any;
    gzip_comp_level 6;
    gzip_types text/plain text/css application/javascript application/json image/svg+xml;
}
```

---

### 🚀 Deploying to Your VPS (Like `logicforge`)

#### Step 1: Build the production bundle
On your local machine in `JavaScriptTrainingCamp`:
```bash
npm run build
```

#### Step 2: Upload files to your VPS
```bash
# Upload web files to /var/www/js_forge
scp -r dist/* root@151.241.99.129:/var/www/js_forge/

# Upload nginx config to sites-available/js_forge
scp deploy/js_forge root@151.241.99.129:/etc/nginx/sites-available/js_forge
```

#### Step 3: Enable the site & reload Nginx
On your VPS terminal:
```bash
ln -sfn /etc/nginx/sites-available/js_forge /etc/nginx/sites-enabled/js_forge
nginx -t && systemctl reload nginx
```

#### Step 4: Issue SSL Certificate with Certbot
```bash
certbot --nginx -d jsforge.staacornews.com -d js-forge.staacornews.com
```

Everything is configured, version-controlled, and ready to deploy whenever you want!

I've implemented both **`🚀 START`** and **`🔄 RESTART`** (plus **`📺 DEMO`**), exactly mirroring the factory-wipe architecture of **`LOGICFORGE`**!

---

### 🕹️ How `<START>` and `<RESTART>` Work

| Control | Action & Behavior |
| :--- | :--- |
| **`🚀 START`** | **Starts or Resumes Campaign**: Switches to Campaign Mode. If you already have saved progress, it picks up right at your active Stage (e.g. Stage 3) with your saved bot and code intact. |
| **`🔄 RESTART`** | **Factory Wipe (LogicForge-Style)**: Opens a cyberpunk warning modal. Confirming it **completely deletes all `localStorage` data** (saved custom robot blueprints, unlocked firmware, sector code, checkpoints) and resets the game back to ground zero on Stage 1 with an **empty assembly cradle and no propulsion**. |
| **`📺 DEMO`** | **Sandbox Mode**: Lets you freely hop into the Demo curriculum sandbox anytime with all tools and sector missions unlocked. |

---

### ⚠️ The LogicForge-Style Confirmation Modal

When you click **`🔄 RESTART`**, an alert sound plays and this modal appears:

```
┌──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  ⚠️ FACTORY RESET // START FRESH                          ✕  │
│                                                              │
│  WIPE SAVED DATA & RESTART?                                  │
│                                                              │
│  Like LOGICFORGE, restarting will completely delete all      │
│  locally stored data:                                        │
│  ❌ Custom Robot Blueprints: All sprites wiped back to blank │
│  ❌ Firmware Upgrades: All systems returned to locked state  │
│  ❌ Saved Code & Checkpoints: Reset to starter templates     │
│  ❌ Assembly Cradle: Rover cleared back to ground zero       │
│                                                              │
│  🚨 CRITICAL WARNING: This action permanently clears your    │
│  browser's localStorage for JS-Forge.                        │
│                                                              │
│  [ CANCEL & KEEP PROGRESS ]   [ 💥 WIPE ALL DATA & RESTART ] │
└──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
```

When confirmed, it executes [`executeFactoryReset()`](file:///home/jack/Desktop/JavaScriptTrainingCamp/src/main.js#L154-L188):
1. Calls `localStorage.removeItem(...)` on all 7 `jsforge_*` storage keys.
2. Clears the fabricator drawing grid and vector rover.
3. Sets `isCradleMode = true` (empty yellow caution cradle).
4. Sets all hardware badges back to ❌ (`CHASSIS`, `PROPULSION`, `STEERING`, `SCANNER`, `AUTOMATION`).
5. Loads **Stage 1: Fabrication Protocol** fresh and logs a factory reset notice to the terminal.
